# Unscheduled Work Register

> **Status**: Active | **Version**: 1.0 | **Created**: 2026-07-28
>
> Companion to the [capability register](./capability-register.md) (what the product must do) and the
> [R1 deferred-debt register](./r1-deferred-debt-register.md) (what R1 knowingly deferred). This
> register holds the third category: work that was **directed in some document but never scheduled
> anywhere**.

## Why this register exists

The 2026-07-28 sweep found a structural gap rather than an oversight. This project maintains four
independent ID namespaces:

| Namespace | Lives in | Mapped into roadmap phases? |
|-----------|----------|------------------------------|
| `K*` / `G*` / `A*` / `S*` | [capability-register.md](./capability-register.md) | 🟡 Mostly, via roadmap.md's "Where every open register item lands" table. Written here as ✅ on 2026-07-28; the linkage check then proved 11 open capabilities (K1, K9, K10, K11, K20, G14, G18, A2, A3, A16, S8) appeared in no row of it. They are now mapped, as unratified proposals |
| `C*` / `GS*` / `U*` / `T*` / `P*` / `SL*` | [r1-deferred-debt-register.md](./r1-deferred-debt-register.md) | ❌ No: zero debt IDs are cited in either master document |
| `AL-*` | [authoring-lessons-log.md](./authoring-lessons-log.md) | ❌ No: zero `AL-` hits in either master document |
| GitHub issues | the tracker | ❌ Partially: 19 of 33 open issues appear in no planning document |

Only the first namespace has phase linkage. This register closes that gap by assigning a phase to
every unmapped item, and by giving items that had no ID at all (ADR follow-ons, design-doc open
questions, code-level deferrals) their first stable identifier.

## How to use it

- **Adding work**: if you defer something, add a `UW-*` row here in the same change, or cite an
  existing register ID. A deferral with no ID and no row is how items got lost before.
- **Doing work**: move the row's Status to `done` and cite the PR. Do not delete rows; the audit
  trail is the point.
- **Dedup discipline**: several docs describe the same findings under different ID schemes. Where a
  row says "canonical", that is the ID to use; the alternatives are cross-referenced, not duplicated.
- **Phase column**: proposed home, not a commitment. `blocked` and `decision` are real states, not
  a scheduling failure; recording that something cannot be scheduled yet is the point.

**Status values**: `unscheduled` (needs a phase commitment) | `blocked` (a named prerequisite is
open) | `decision` (waits on an owner ruling) | `verify` (may already be done; confirm then close) |
`done` (closed, with a PR reference).

---

## The linkage contract

This section is the enforced contract, not advice. `scripts/check_work_linkage.py` reads it as the
specification and fails the build on a violation. Changing the rules means changing that script and
its tests, deliberately, in the same change.

### The invariant

Every row in every register must resolve to exactly one **disposition**:

| Disposition | Means | Required evidence |
|-------------|-------|-------------------|
| scheduled | has a home in the plan | `Phase` holds a value from the vocabulary below |
| `blocked` | cannot be scheduled yet | the row names the open prerequisite |
| `decision` | waits on a human ruling | the row names the owner who must rule |
| `verify` | may already be done | the row names what to check |
| `done` | closed | the row cites a PR, commit, or issue |

A row with a status but no evidence, or with a `Phase` value outside the vocabulary, is an
**orphan**. Orphan count is the health metric for this whole system: it goes to zero and a check
keeps it there. Everything else here is plumbing in service of that one number.

This generalizes the rule `scripts/check_lessons_log.py` already enforces on the lessons log: a
status asserts something happened, so it must cite what proves it.

### Phase vocabulary (closed set)

| Group | Allowed values | Source of truth |
|-------|----------------|-----------------|
| Product phases | `0` `1` `2` `2b` `3` `4a` `4b` `4c` `4d` `5` | `roadmap.md` `## Phase` headings |
| Track 2 phases | `6` `7` `8` `9` | `PROJECT-PLAN.md` Track 2 |
| Milestones | `M0`..`M7`, and dotted sub-milestones such as `M4.1` | `roadmap.md` Milestones |
| Release rungs | `R1` `R2` `R3` | `roadmap.md` release ladder |
| Named workstreams | `content` | `roadmap.md` Content workstream |
| Queue | `now` | `roadmap.md` Now queue |

### Non-phase dispositions (closed set)

Not every piece of directed work belongs to a product phase. These sentinels are legitimate, and
being a closed set is what stops them becoming a junk drawer:

| Sentinel | For |
|----------|-----|
| `CI hygiene` | repo tooling and gates; no product phase |
| `doc` | a documentation correction with no code change |
| `recurring` | an ongoing practice, not a one-time deliverable |
| `post-launch` | deliberately deferred past R3 |
| `external:<repo>` | owned by another repository, for example `external:homelab-infra` |
| `issue:<number>` | a live defect tracked in the issue tracker rather than the phase plan, for example `issue:460` |

### Not allowed

- A cross-reference in the `Phase` column (`see G`, `see H`). Point at a real phase, or set the
  status to `blocked` and name the blocker. "See another cluster" is not a disposition.
- An empty `Phase` on a row whose status is `unscheduled`.
- The same ID in two namespaces.
- More than one value in the `Phase` column. Work that spans phases takes the **earliest** phase,
  where it starts, and says so in the Item text. A comma-list hides which phase owns the commitment,
  which is the failure this register exists to prevent.
- The `Phase` column repeating the `Status` value. `decision` and `blocked` are statuses; a row in
  either state still needs the phase it will land in once resolved.

### How the other three registers link in

They keep their own schemas, because they track different things and flattening them would lose
information. This register is the **join table** that gives their rows a phase:

| Register | Linkage obligation |
|----------|--------------------|
| [capability-register.md](./capability-register.md) | a row not marked ✅ must appear in `roadmap.md`'s "Where every open register item lands" mapping |
| [r1-deferred-debt-register.md](./r1-deferred-debt-register.md) | a row not marked `[Closed]` or `[Resolved]` must be cited by a `UW-B*` row here. That register uses both markers interchangeably (3 and 7 rows respectively as of 2026-07-28); the validator honours both, because treating `[Resolved]` rows as open would report closed work as a gap and train readers to ignore the check |
| [authoring-lessons-log.md](./authoring-lessons-log.md) | a lesson whose status is not `applied`, `rejected`, or `superseded` must be cited by a `UW-C*` row here |

That is why clusters B and C exist. They are not commentary; they are the linkage layer, and the
validator treats a missing entry there as a build failure.

### Validate

```bash
uv run python scripts/check_work_linkage.py
```

| UW-A01 | **Tiered RLS scoping in its entirety**: Tier-1 scoped policies, `set_config` per request, the spike, and the integration suite run as `cyo_api`. Zero references in any planning document. | 022 | 5 | unscheduled |
| UW-A02 | Demote any Tier-1 table lacking a flat `family_id`; author the denormalization migration | 022 | 5 | unscheduled |
| UW-A03 | **Production cutover** (issue #559): retire `postgres`-role traffic. **Closed 2026-08-04, verified live against production** (`cvrnaydpzijtszfbsraq`): `pg_authid` shows `cyo_api`/`cyo_worker` both `LOGIN`, password set, `rolbypassrls=f`; `pg_stat_activity` shows 5 active app connections as `cyo_api` and **zero** as `postgres`, so the switch is observed in traffic, not just provisioned. Sequenced safely: PR #560 (`eb96f687`, sets Tier 1 RLS context before the device-grant lookup) was merged and re-verified green on `e2e-staging.yml` (multiple 2026-08-03 runs) before the homelab team rotated the production passwords, so the cutover did not reproduce the device-grant 401 that first surfaced on staging. The one leg left unconfirmed by that snapshot, `cyo_worker` showing 0 active connections because workers connect on demand rather than continuously, is now closed **two independent ways, both 2026-08-04**. First, a read-only probe run inside the live `cyo-adventure-worker` container through the application's own `get_worker_session()` returned `current_user` and `session_user` both `cyo_worker`, with `rolbypassrls = false`, and `pg_stat_activity` read from that same connection showed `cyo_api` on 5, `cyo_worker` on 1, and `postgres` on none; confirming the credential path directly made triggering a real generation job unnecessary, so no LLM quota was spent. Second, PR #608 replaced that one-off check with a standing one: the worker probes its own engine at startup and logs `generation_worker.role_least_privileged` or `generation_worker.role_bypasses_rls`, so from here on the leg is a log read after a worker restart rather than a `pg_stat_activity` snapshot timed to a live job. Read the fields, not the event name: `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` is unset on the deployed worker, so `worker_database_url_effective` falls back to `database_url`, and `worker_dsn_explicitly_set: false` is the field that identifies that state. One correction to what #608 assumed about that fallback, established by the live probe above and recorded here because the two landed within a day of each other: the fallback does **not** resolve to the API's role. `DATABASE_URL` is per container, and the homelab stack sets the worker container's own `DATABASE_URL` to the `cyo_worker` DSN, so the affirmative event is emitted as `cyo_worker`, reaching the right role by a different mechanism than the `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` split that `docker-compose.prod.yml` configures. Either way the outcome is least-privileged and the underlying finding is unchanged: the role is right by fallback rather than by design, which leaves `get_session()` and `get_worker_session()` indistinguishable inside the worker process. That is registered once, as `UW-A44` landing with PR #609; do not open a second row for it here. Recorded in the [R1 live e2e checklist](./r1-live-e2e-checklist.md) sign-off for 2026-08-04. The ADR names M4.1 as its review gate; M4.1 carries no ADR-021 content. | 021 | M4.1 | done |

| UW-A43 | **Bind a child session to its minting device grant.** Revoking a device grant stops the DEVICE token but not a child session already minted from it: `api/deps.py::_child_principal` does no database round-trip and the token carries no grant reference, so a child keeps reading online for the rest of `child_session_ttl_seconds` (12 hours). Fix: carry the grant `jti` in the child-session claims and check it on the read path, accepting a database read there. Disclosed in ADR-014 "Negative / risks"; current behavior pinned by `tests/integration/test_child_sessions.py::test_known_limitation_revoked_device_grant_does_not_invalidate_minted_child_session`. | 014 | 5 | unscheduled |
| UW-A44 | **ADR-021's role split is enforced by container environment, not by the code path written for it, so a `get_session()` regression in worker code would be invisible in production.** Found 2026-08-04 while confirming the last open leg of `UW-A03`. The worker reaches the right role: `current_user` inside `cyo-adventure-worker` is `cyo_worker`. But it gets there by fallback, not by design. `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` is **unset** on the deployed container, so `worker_database_url_effective` falls back to `database_url`, and the homelab stack (`services/cyo-adventure/` in `homelab-infra`) happens to set that container's own `DATABASE_URL` to the `cyo_worker` DSN. This repository's `docker-compose.prod.yml` instead sets `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` explicitly, as commit `629a0263` intended, so the deployed stack and the committed compose file reach the same outcome by different mechanisms. Two consequences. First, inside the worker process both engines resolve to the same identity, so the `get_worker_session()` discipline that `core/database.py` documents as load-bearing is currently a no-op in production, and worker code that mistakenly called `get_session()` would still connect as `cyo_worker` and pass every observable check. Second, whichever mechanism is chosen should be the one that is tested, because right now neither file is authoritative. PR #608 lands a startup role probe that logs `generation_worker.role_least_privileged` or `generation_worker.role_bypasses_rls`, which is a real improvement to observability but does **not** close this row, and the reason is the whole point of it: that probe reports the role the worker connected as, and the role is `cyo_worker` whether it arrived by the intended split or by the container-env fallback, so it cannot distinguish the two and cannot see a `get_session()` regression in worker code at all. Its `worker_dsn_explicitly_set: false` field is the part that reads on this finding. **To do:** decide whether the container env or `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` is the contract, make the other match it, and add a test that fails when a worker entry point uses the API session factory (a static check over `generation/worker*.py` and `covers/worker.py` is enough, and unlike any live or startup probe it keeps working when the two roles coincide, which today they do). | 021 | 5 | unscheduled |
| UW-A45 | **Nothing ties the emitted `schema_version` stamp to `SCHEMA_VERSION`, so ADR-025 decision 2's stamping clause and decision 3's converse are unenforced.** Found 2026-08-06 during the final whole-branch review of the ADR-025 implementation (branch `feat/persistent-characters`). `Storybook.schema_version` defaults to `SCHEMA_VERSION` only when the field is absent from the input; every real producer supplies it explicitly and hardcodes `"2.0"` (the 61 committed skeleton documents under `skeletons/`; note `skeletons/*/*.json` matches 124 files, the other 63 being `.contract.json`/`.lineage.json` sidecars on a different versioning scheme, `scripts/build_series_book.py`, `scripts/seed_dev_data.py`, `scripts/seed_series_catalog.py`). Outside `_normalize_legacy_fill`, no *Python* in `src/` ever assigns `blob["schema_version"]`, but that scan misses the primary production path: the Stage-A prompt (`generation/templates/structure.md:27`) requires the model to emit `schema_version` and pins no value, so an LLM-generated skeleton carries whatever the model chose. That producer, not the hardcoded scripts, is the one this row's fix most needs to cover. (`scripts/build_series_book.py:315` is also not a hardcode: it reads `spec.get("schema_version", "2.0")`.) So decision 2's "It stamps newly-published blobs with the current version" and decision 3's converse (a story only carries a field defined at the minor it was published at) both hold today only because no minor beyond `0` has ever existed, not because anything enforces them. Recorded rather than fixed in this branch, since rewriting every producer was out of scope for a schema-versioning fix. **Owner ruling given 2026-08-06: neither a publish-path stamper nor a hand-update sweep. Enforce the converse instead, with a validator rule requiring a document's declared minor to cover the fields it actually uses:** if a document uses any field introduced at minor N, its `schema_version` must be `>= N`. This is deterministic, needs no producer changes, and leaves a plain `2.0` skeleton correctly stamped `2.0` rather than force-bumping every catalog file on each minor. It enforces decision 3 (a story only carries fields defined at its published minor) directly and makes decision 2's stamping clause unnecessary for correctness: an under-declared document fails the gate instead of being silently admitted. Scheduled into the ADR-025 follow-on plan alongside the `SCHEMA_MINOR` bump to `1`; this row closes when that rule ships with tests. **Implemented (2026-08-06) on `feat/persistent-characters` (PR #636)**, which delivers the declared-minor-covers-used-fields validator rule per the owner ruling above. Flips to `done` when that rule (L1-8) ships with tests. **Shipped on this branch 2026-08-06**: `L1-8` (`src/cyo_adventure/validator/layer1.py::_check_field_minors`, registry `src/cyo_adventure/storybook/field_minors.py`) with tests in `tests/unit/test_field_minor_floor.py`, catalogued in `docs/planning/validator-rules.md`. **Closed 2026-08-07**: PR [#636](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/636) squash-merged to `main` as `4fc65e5b`, so L1-8 and its tests are on `main` and the citation survives the squash. | 025 | 5 | done |
| UW-A46 | **Pathfinder Phase 0 pilot: one participating skeleton in a 13-16 gamebook cell, built to [ADR-028](./adr/adr-028-persistent-reader-characters.md)'s `accepts_character` envelope and proven by the CH-* validator rules.** Owner ruled GO on SQ-22 / gate OG5 (2026-08-05); see [story-structure-improvement-plan.md](./story-structure-improvement-plan.md) SQ-22/OG5 and [pathfinder-structure-exploration.md](./pathfinder-structure-exploration.md) section 8. **Unblocked 2026-08-07**: the CH-* rules landed on `main` in `4fc65e5b` (PR [#636](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/636)), so the only remaining work is authoring the pilot skeleton itself. **Scope note (2026-08-08)**: this row covers the pilot skeleton and its A/B measurement **only**. It does not cover the persistent-characters *runtime* (binding, replay, progression writeback, CRUD/API, UI), which is a separate body of work on branch `feat/persistent-characters-runtime`; an earlier revision of ADR-028 wrongly said this row was closed by that branch. **First attempt withdrawn 2026-08-08**: a 13-16 stat-envelope pilot was drafted and pulled from the branch rather than shipped, because with `_check_dead_branches` envelope-blind every stat gate had to use `AL-129`'s XOR workaround, which leaves the reader unable to perceive the stat deciding anything and made all 14 stations mechanically identical. It is preserved out of tree. **Second withdrawal, same day**: the 8-11 archetype pilot (`the-storm-chasers-club`, promoted to Tier 2 on `feat/persistent-characters-runtime`) was withdrawn on 2026-08-08 for an unrelated mechanism, a tautological archetype gate that passed every CH-* rule (`AL-131`), so the shipped pilot turned out to be **as imperceptible as the withdrawn one** and neither pilot demonstrated a reader-visible envelope. Two pilots built to the same spec failed observability by two independent routes, which is evidence about the spec and the gate rather than about either book. **Preservation risk, stated plainly: both withdrawn skeletons exist ONLY in gitignored locations today** (`.superpowers/sdd/held/`, which `.gitignore:323` excludes, plus a session scratchpad under `/tmp`). Neither is in git history on any branch: the withdrawal commit `e16b4db4` removed the 8-11 promotion and the 13-16 pilot was never committed at all, so `git log`/`git show` cannot recover either one. A `git clean -fdx`, a scratchpad expiry, or a machine change destroys both with no warning and no recovery path. A tracked home would be the two skeleton JSON documents committed under a non-catalog path that the gate and the catalog loader do not scan (the catalog reads `skeletons/<band>/*.json`, so a sibling directory such as `skeletons/withdrawn/` or an equivalent under `docs/`), each paired with a short note recording why it was withdrawn and which lesson (`AL-129` for the 13-16 stat pilot, `AL-131` for the 8-11 archetype pilot) explains the mechanism. **That is a scope decision for the owner and is deliberately not taken here**: re-adding the files reverses a withdrawal that was itself a reviewed decision, so it needs an owner ruling rather than a docs fix. Until that ruling, treat both artifacts as unrecoverable. This row therefore stays open, and whether authoring a pilot is worthwhile at all now depends on the envelope-awareness decision carried by `UW-C64`. | 028 | 5 | unscheduled |
| UW-A47 | **A character-enabled book holds a synchronous `run_gate` call for tens of seconds against a fixed-size 40-thread pool, and nothing bounds how many can be in flight at once.** Found 2026-08-06 while re-measuring the two `#CRITICAL: timing` markers on `feat/persistent-characters` (PR [#636](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/636)). Measured on the `longwinter-station` catalog skeleton (248 nodes, 51,241 base configurations): `run_gate` takes 0.77s with no envelope and **49.58s** with the canonical 27-state stat envelope, a ~64x multiplier, because CH-3b and CH-4 re-walk Layer 2 once per entry state. Both `api/node_edit.py` and `api/generation.py` offload via `await run_sync(run_gate, ...)` onto AnyIO's default worker-thread pool (40 threads), and both routes are guardian-reachable. A book this size passing the CH rules is admissible, not an error case, so CH-5 and CH-8 do not catch it: they reject envelopes that are too large or graphs that are too expensive to expand, and this one is neither. Forty concurrent gate calls on comparable books exhaust the pool for roughly a minute, at which point every other `run_sync` caller in the process (not just these two routes) queues behind them. Recorded rather than fixed here because the fix is an architectural choice, not a tuning constant. **To do:** pick one of (a) move `run_gate` for character-enabled books onto the existing RQ queue so it never occupies a request thread, (b) give the gate its own bounded `CapacityLimiter` so saturation degrades these two routes instead of the whole process, or (c) memoise the per-entry-state Layer 2 walk, whose cost is dominated by re-deriving the same configuration set 27 times. Whichever is chosen, add a test that fails when a guardian-reachable route can hold a worker thread past a stated budget. **Closed (Task 10 of the persistent-reader-characters workstream, ADR-028, branch `feat/persistent-characters-runtime`): took option (b).** A single, process-wide `anyio.CapacityLimiter` (`api/gate_limits.py::gate_limiter()`, sized from the new `gate_max_concurrency` setting, default 4) now bounds both call sites via `limiter=gate_limiter()`; the two `#CRITICAL: timing` markers keep the 49.58s per-call measurement and additionally state the hold is bounded and by how much. This is concurrency, not latency: the 49.58s worst case is unchanged, so a queued request still holds its HTTP connection and may time out. **Proof corrected 2026-08-08.** As first written, this row cited `tests/unit/test_gate_capacity_limiter.py` as proof, but a whole-branch review found that two of those three tests passed unchanged with the limiter removed from both call sites, so the row asserted a proof it did not have; a test that cannot fail is not evidence, and a register row citing one launders it into the record. All three were rewritten to discriminate, and each was break-tested by deleting the property it names and confirming the failure. `test_both_gate_call_sites_share_one_limiter` now parses the AST of `api/node_edit.py` and `api/generation.py` and asserts each passes `limiter=gate_limiter()` to its own `run_sync(run_gate, ...)` call, rather than only that the singleton accessor memoises, which was true with or without a caller. `test_calls_beyond_the_limit_queue_rather_than_spawn` uses a `threading.Barrier` sized to `total_tokens + 1`: a correctly bounded limiter can never supply the extra party, so the barrier times out while the limiter holds and trips as soon as it is removed. `test_the_gate_limiter_is_smaller_than_the_anyio_default_pool` compares against AnyIO's live default pool size instead of a hardcoded 40, and asserts the `le=39` bound on `gate_max_concurrency` (`core/config.py:988`) is enforced by pydantic rather than merely documented. Options (a) and (c) were not taken; (c), memoising the per-entry-state Layer 2 walk, is the only one of the three that attacks the 64x multiplier itself and is split out as `UW-A48`. | 028 | 5 | done |
| UW-A48 | **Memoise the per-entry-state Layer 2 walk that drives `run_gate`'s ~64x character-envelope multiplier.** Split from `UW-A47`, which bounded concurrency (a shared `CapacityLimiter`, `api/gate_limits.py`) but deliberately left per-call latency unchanged: the 49.58s worst case on `skeletons/16+/the-longwinter-station.json` (248 nodes, 51,241 base configurations) with the canonical 27-state `accepts_character` envelope, against 0.77s with none, still stands. CH-3b and CH-4 re-walk Layer 2 once per entry state, re-deriving the same configuration set 27 times; caching that walk (or its derived configuration set) across entry states is the fix option `UW-A47` recorded as (c) and did not take. | UW-A49 | **ADR-028's accepted residual: the bound seed can disagree with the seed the client opened from, and nothing reports it.** Recorded as an accepted residual under ADR-028's "Integrity posture" section but given no register row, issue, or phase until now, which `docs/planning/adr/README.md` forbids for a consequent of an Accepted ADR. The mechanism: the active character can change between the client's seed fetch and `_bind_active_character`'s call on the create path (`src/cyo_adventure/api/reading.py`), so the reading-state row is created carrying character B's `seed_var_state` while the `var_state` the client sends was produced from character A's. Nothing rejects it, because rejection needs a replay proof, replay needs a `choice_path`, and the shipped client sends none; a first save omitting `choice_path` is checked only against the structural floor. Two consequences, both recorded in the `#EDGE` marker on `_bind_active_character` in `api/reading.py`, which is the authority. First, user-visible today: on the read's next resume `canGoBack` fails closed against the mismatched seed, so **Go back silently disappears for the rest of that read** and RESTART reopens from the new character's numbers. Second, latent and worse: anything that starts sending `choice_path` (a native client, the offline queue, replay-based conflict resolution) converts this from a degraded Go-back button into a **permanently wedged read**, because every later save then replays from the stored seed, disagrees, and 422s. A related residual covers a fresh read that cannot reach the network, or is served a service-worker-cached `characters` response with no `seed_var_state`: it opens from declared initials. **To do**: decide the reporting posture before anything sends `choice_path`, since the escalation is triggered by that change rather than by time. The candidate fixes are (a) re-read the active character at bind time and reject or re-seed on a mismatch, (b) return the bound seed to the client on the create response so a divergence is detectable client-side, or (c) accept the degradation permanently and make it visible rather than silent. Blocks nothing on this branch; must be closed before `UW-C19` (making the client send `choice_path`) lands, and `UW-C72` is the other row that escalates on the same trigger. | 028 | 5 | unscheduled |

At the oldest band the median whole-graph artifact is roughly 42 times the words on the path a child reads (median 42,700, maximum 118,475, against about 2,800), so "a few minutes" is achievable only as flagged passages plus sampling, which the ADR does not say. | UW-A55 | **`ExternalServiceError` has no status of its own, so a KWS outage and a permanent misconfiguration are the same 400 to the browser.** `app.py::_STATUS_BY_EXCEPTION` lists six exception types and `ExternalServiceError` is not among them, so it falls through to the 400 fallback alongside `ConfigurationError` and `BusinessLogicError`. On `POST /api/v1/consent/kws/start` that collapses two opposite situations: three permanent refusals (KWS unconfigured on this tier, no `User` row, no email on file) where retrying cannot help, and a vendor outage or timeout where retrying is exactly the right advice. `GuardianVerificationPage.tsx::messageForStartError` must pick one message for the merged code and picks "Trying again will not help, so please contact support", which is wrong advice for the outage case; PR #681 sharpens the cost by resolving a failed send to `send_failed`, so the retry the parent is told not to attempt would now be accepted immediately. Deliberately not fixed inline: giving `ExternalServiceError` a 502 is a repo-wide contract change touching every router that raises it, and the alternative (a machine-readable rule code in the error body) is a response-shape change with its own client-generation consequences. The failure direction chosen meanwhile is the safe one for a COPPA gate, since the opposite default loops a parent forever against a condition only an operator can clear. Marked with a `#ASSUME`/`#VERIFY` pair at the mapping site. **Closed by PR #699** (branch `fix/uw-a55-external-service-502`): `app.py::_STATUS_BY_EXCEPTION` now carries `(ExternalServiceError, 502)`; `APIError`, `DatabaseError`, and `ProviderError` (its only subclasses, none raised at a route boundary except `ExternalServiceError` itself today) inherit 502 through that one row via `isinstance` rather than needing rows of their own. The repo-wide audit the deferral called for found exactly two direct `raise ExternalServiceError` sites reachable from a route: `api/covers.py::request_cover` (an unreachable RQ broker) and `consent/kws_client.py` (every KWS send/auth failure, surfaced through `consent/service.py` to `api/consent.py::start_kws_verification`); 502 is correct at both, since both are genuinely retryable vendor/infra failures with no case where the old 400 was load-bearing. Both routes' `error_responses()` declarations were widened to document 502 (`api/covers.py` gained it alongside its existing 400; `api/consent.py` gained it alongside 400/403/409/429), and `api/schemas.py::_ERROR_DESCRIPTIONS` gained a 502 entry, which changes `app.openapi()`'s output for both operations. The committed frontend client (`frontend/src/client/`) was regenerated in the same commit against a schema dumped in-process the way `ci.yml`'s `contract` job dumps it, so no live backend was needed and the drift job has nothing to find. The frontend RAD marker on `CANNOT_SEND` in `GuardianVerificationPage.tsx` is resolved and rewritten to describe the closed state rather than the open assumption; `messageForStartError` needed no new branch, since `classifyApiError` already treats any status >= 500 (502 included) as its `server` kind and the existing override already returns the retry-appropriate `START_ERROR` copy, so a 502 was correct by construction once the backend stopped returning 400 for it. Tests updated: `tests/unit/test_app.py` (`ExternalServiceError`/`DatabaseError`/`APIError`/`ProviderError` all now assert 502), `tests/integration/test_consent_api.py` (two tests asserting the old 400 now assert 502), `tests/integration/test_cover_api.py` (tightened a `>= 400` assertion to `== 502`), and `frontend/src/auth/GuardianVerificationPage.test.tsx` (new case pinning 502 to the retry-appropriate copy, distinct from the 400/403 permanent-refusal case). Two consequences of the new status found in review and fixed in the same PR: (a) the 502 body was disclosing `details.service_name` and `details.status_code`, naming the upstream vendor and its HTTP status to the caller, so both keys joined `app.py::_SENSITIVE_DETAIL_KEYS` and are now server-log-only like `value` and `context`; (b) `consent/kws_client.py` was collapsing an operator-fixable credential rejection (401/403 from either KWS leg: a bad client id or API key, a lost grant, or the missing `User-Agent` KWS answers with a 403 "Request blocked") into the same retryable 502 as a transient outage, reproducing the "please try again" loop this row set out to end, so those statuses now raise `ConfigurationError` and land on the existing operator-problem 400 alongside "KWS is not configured". That also narrows the status oracle: 400 no longer distinguishes "unconfigured" from "configured but rejecting our credentials". | 018 | 5 | done |
| UW-A56 | **Two designs exist for ADR-018's 16 CFR 312.5(c) consent record, and the narrower one shipped by default rather than by decision.** What is live on `main` is `kws_verification` (migration `20260809130000`), a KWS-specific table: status set `sent`/`verified`/`failed`, vendor config captured as a single `enabled_methods` JSONB, one resolution-pairing constraint, and no column naming which verification method produced the row. | UW-C33 | `AL-100` The frontend format gate is scoped to `src/**`, not the tree: `format:check` runs `prettier --check "src/**/*.{ts,tsx,css,json}"` (frontend/package.json), so `e2e/`, `e2e-real/`, `eslint.config.js`, `index.html`, and `README.md` are unchecked, and `npx prettier --check .` reports 130 unformatted files under `frontend/`. This is the TypeScript twin of the Python gap PR #635 closed with the `format-tree` job, which deliberately does not cover it (ruff does not format TypeScript). The gap has TWO loci, not one: the pre-commit hook `frontend-prettier` is scoped the same way (`files: ^frontend/src/` plus the same four extensions), so staging an `e2e-real` spec reports "no files to check" and passes. Fixing only the npm script leaves the hook blind. Widen both, honouring `.prettierignore` (which already exempts `src/client/`, `dist/`, `coverage/`, `playwright-report/`, `test-results/`), and land the resulting mechanical reformat as its own commit so it is reviewable as formatting rather than mixed into a behaviour change. **Two corrections to this row, both measured 2026-08-24 in a clean worktree off `origin/main` at `b97c7cbf`.** (a) **Both counts are correct, and their disagreement is itself the finding.** 53 is what a clean checkout reports; 130 is what any checkout that has run design-sync reports, because the balance are generated files under `frontend/design-system/` (`ds-bundle/`, `.ds-sync/`, `.design-sync/.cache/`). Those are gitignored, but by `frontend/design-system/.gitignore`, and Prettier's default `--ignore-path` is `['.gitignore', '.prettierignore']` resolved against the CWD only: it never reads a nested `.gitignore`. The original 130 was therefore not a dirty checkout in the sense of uncommitted edits, and dismissing it as unreproducible was wrong. Measured both ways on 2026-08-24 during the PR #754 review: 0 unformatted in a fresh worktree, 78 on the same commit with the design-sync artefacts materialised. Zero of the 53 tracked files are under `src/`, which is the expected shape: the old gate already held `src/` clean, so the unformatted set is exactly the set the gate could not see. (b) The row's list of missed paths is incomplete in the way that matters most. Beyond `e2e/`, `e2e-real/`, `eslint.config.js`, `index.html` and `README.md`, **26 of the 53 are in `frontend/design-system/`**, a separate npm package (`@cyo/design-system`) reached by the app through the `@ds` alias in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.app.json`. It is not a workspace of `frontend/package.json`, so no glob rooted there ever reached it. Its own CI job (`ci.yml:357`) runs typecheck, test and build and **has no lint or format step at all**, which makes it a second, wider scope hole than the one this row was opened for: see the companion lint finding, since `frontend/package.json`'s `lint` script does not name it either and the design-system package has no `lint` script, so its 44 files and 1,292 lines are linted by nothing in CI. **Fixed on unpushed branch `feat/frontend-assurance-gaps`**, two commits as this row asks: `d39a8a8d` widens both loci (npm scripts to `prettier --check .`, hook `files:` to `^frontend/.*` over Prettier-supported extensions only, since the hook passes explicit paths and Prettier exits non-zero rather than skipping on an unparseable one like `nginx.conf`), and `471c7acd` carries the 53-file reformat alone. Exclusions were left to `.prettierignore` rather than duplicated into the glob, which works because Prettier honours that file even for explicitly-passed paths; `package-lock.json` was added to it on the existing `src/client/` rule that generated output is formatted by its generator. **That exclusion set was initially incomplete**, per (a): it named only the artefacts `frontend/.prettierignore` could see, so `format:check` was green in CI and red on any machine that had run design-sync, and `npm run format` would have rewritten ~2.6 MB of generated output including the 1,111,175-byte vendored `design-system/ds-bundle/_vendor/react.js`. The four design-sync output directories were added to `frontend/.prettierignore` in the PR #754 review pass and the fix was verified 78 to 0 on the PR branch with the artefacts present. Discrimination was proven rather than assumed: the widened hook fails on `frontend/e2e-real/admin-management-real.spec.ts`, a path the old `^frontend/src/` pattern provably never matched, so it is not passing on an empty file list. Note the reformat is **not** whitespace-only, because `.prettierrc` sets `semi: false` and `singleQuote: true`. Verified green after it: frontend lint (unchanged single pre-existing warning), `tsc -b`, 2384 vitest tests, `npm run build` with the `check-sw-denylist` postbuild guard, and the design-system package's own typecheck, 72 tests, build and `react-stack-top-frame` bundle guard. Status stays `unscheduled` on the `UW-C74` precedent: the branch is unmerged and unpushed, so the gate on `main` is still the narrow one. Closes when that branch merges. | 4b | unscheduled |

| UW-C105 | **Canonical** for the sibling-fill gate-revise-regate loop; `UW-C147` is the same finding under `AL-215` and cross-references this row. `AL-169` The gate-revise-regate fill loop closed a 20.4 to 1.2 per-1000 gram gap in one agentic round with no human repair. Original ask: wire `check_sibling_fills` (plus title and menu checks) into the generation fill loop as a blocking gate with an automated revision pass, capped rounds before human flag. **Partly satisfied 2026-08-23, and the residual is narrower than the original ask.** The anti-template guard now runs inside the moderation pipeline (`moderation/leaf_diversity.py`, wired at `moderation/pipeline.py`), emitting one `Verdict.FLAG` per templated node on `FAIL`, which routes the book into the pipeline's existing bounded repair and then to the human gate; self-repetition and narrative-person drift ship alongside it as `Verdict.ADVISORY` (`moderation/prose_craft.py`). **Three things the original ask names are still missing, and each is a separate reason this cannot be closed.** (1) It is not a blocking gate: the ATG is advisory-plus-soft-flag by contract and never rejects. (2) There is one repair round, not capped rounds: the pipeline's single bounded repair is not a revise-regate loop, and adding rounds means re-running the gate after each, which nothing does today. (3) The menu-frame check (`check_sibling_fills.py::menu_frame_overlap`, the recognition channel `AL-161` identified) has no request-path counterpart at all. **A fourth item closed 2026-08-23**: the sibling-gram measure reached the request path when [#742](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/742) merged as `37a08a60`, so `diversity/grams.py` is now the one definition `moderation/leaf_diversity.py` and `scripts/check_sibling_fills.py` both read; they differ only in `include_choice_labels`, which is a deliberate difference of question rather than drift. Promotion of any of the advisories to a gate additionally needs a threshold distribution measured across real books, which no run has produced; picking a cut-off without one is how an advisory becomes a false-reject engine. Depends on `UW-C315`'s unresolved lever decision for what a blocking threshold could even be set to. | now | unscheduled |

| UW-C116 | `AL-180` The strict authoring bar passes 2 of 61 catalog skeletons (0 of 11 at 10-13) and the pilots' control skeleton is a non-production-eligible off-matrix seed the selector never serves. Decide whether the strict bar is the production bar and re-baseline diversity work on a production-eligible skeleton in a real cell. **Re-measured 2026-08-22** by running `check_skeleton.py` over all 84 shells with and without `--strict`: 20 of 84 pass strict, not 2 of 61, so the cost is retiring **74% of the production-eligible catalog (55 of 74), not 97%**. Two findings sharpen the decision. First, the gate that promotion actually runs is not in question: only 3 of 84 shells fail without `--strict`, and all three are already `production_eligible: false`, so nothing a request can reach fails today. Second, **the delta is entirely the `CG-*` grammar family**, which `--strict` promotes from advisory to blocking: of the 55 eligible shells strict would reject, 21 fail on `CG-*` alone and 34 fail on `CG-*` plus something structural, while **zero fail on structural grounds alone** (`CG-3` accounts for 1,557 of the findings, almost all of it the words-per-stop ceiling). So the question to rule on is whether `CG-*` is calibrated for the catalog we ship, not whether the structural floors are right. | now | unscheduled |
| UW-C117 | `AL-181` Length demand concentrates on medium while the catalog is flat at 3 to 4 per cell, and cells are hard partitions (0 of 58 production skeletons carry a wildcard length), so short and long surplus is stranded and the medium cell binds in every band. Author against the demand curve: first 6 to 12 skeletons per band go entirely to its medium cell, and measure the real length distribution to replace the assumed regimes. | now | unscheduled |
| UW-C118 | `AL-182` Kid auto-approve hardcodes length=SHORT for every band, steering unchosen requests into the thinnest-demand tier and producing a guaranteed 422 for teen-band children because 13-16 and 16+ have no short cell by design; ADR-011 band-by-length is also unvalidated at intake so a long 3-5 or short 13-16 request fails late. Two findings sharpen the decision. First, the gate that promotion actually runs is not in question: only 3 of 84 shells fail without `--strict`, and all three are already `production_eligible: false`, so nothing a request can reach fails today. Second, **the delta is entirely the `CG-*` grammar family**, which `--strict` promotes from advisory to blocking: of the 55 eligible shells strict would reject, 21 fail on `CG-*` alone and 34 fail on `CG-*` plus something structural, while **zero fail on structural grounds alone** (`CG-3` accounts for 1,557 of the findings, almost all of it the words-per-stop ceiling). So the question to rule on is whether `CG-*` is calibrated for the catalog we ship, not whether the structural floors are right. | now | unscheduled |

| UW-C256 | `AL-378` W7's inter-judge agreement is computed by rounding each judge's mean across all seven criteria to an integer and running Cohen's kappa on the result. That discards the per-criterion structure W7 exists to examine, and lands in the skewed-marginals regime where kappa collapses despite high raw agreement: gpt-5.6 rounds into two categories with 24 of 31 books in one, grok-4.6 into three with 23 of 31 in one. The reported figures (+0.16, +0.58, +0.14, all below the 0.60 floor) therefore measure how differently spread each judge's scale is, not whether they agree, and must not be quoted. Compute agreement per criterion on within-book deltas with an ordinal-appropriate statistic (weighted kappa or a rank correlation), and report the marginal distributions beside it. **Done 2026-08-15 (`AL-403`)**, recomputed from the 129 surviving per-judge verdict records into `scripts/w7_agreement.py` and `out/w7/agreement.txt`: Spearman over within-book deltas for agreement about what a defect did, quadratic-weighted kappa over raw scores for agreement about level, marginals printed beside both. The corrected picture is criterion-specific and inverts the pooled reading: `imagery` strong (rho +0.49 to +0.76, qwk +0.70 to +0.82), `age_fit` good (rho +0.45 to +0.70), `voice` poor (rho +0.04 to +0.27), and on `choice_quality` gemini-3.1 correlates NEGATIVELY with both others (-0.15, -0.02) while gpt-5.6 and grok-4.6 agree at +0.46. gemini-3.1 is the outlier on four of seven criteria and uses the full 1 to 5 range where the others compress into 2 to 4, independently confirming the earlier 2-to-6x noise-floor drop from excluding it. The +0.16 / +0.58 / +0.14 figures remain retracted. | now | done |

Each occurrence was recorded as a limitation of the blocked item rather than as one recurring cause. Invert the default: a paid harness writes to a tracked path unless explicitly told otherwise, and its report states what was discarded. The measure that matters is not what a run cost to produce but what it would cost to reproduce. **Done 2026-08-15 (`AL-404`), with the diagnosis corrected**: `out/vendor-comparison` was never gitignored and neither harness has an output default, so "invert the default" addressed a mechanism that was not operating. The output was simply never committed and nothing said so. `scripts/_paid_output.py` now refuses a gitignored destination before any provider call and prints the cost, the unreproducibility, and the exact commit command after one; wired into `w7_battery.py` and `judge_books.py` with `--allow-untracked-out` for scratch runs. | now | done |
| UW-C258 | `AL-380` The quality panel's run-to-run variation on an unchanged book reaches a full scale point, against a 0.5 detection margin, so a W7 verdict near the margin is not reproducible: three moved between the two 2026-08-14 runs (`engagement` KEEP to INCONCLUSIVE, `choice_quality` 5/5 to 4/5, `voice` 2/6 to 1/6) with no change to the arms. Two independent noise estimates agree in size (0.29 to 0.77 between judges on controls, 0.33 to 1.00 between runs), so the control-noise floor is measuring something real. Score each arm more than once and report the spread; at about $2.60 per 129 scorings this is a budget decision rather than a design obstacle. Until then, mark any verdict whose median effect sits within one noise floor of the margin as provisional. `imagery`, `age_fit` and `ending_quality` survived the change of run and are unaffected. **Partly measured 2026-08-15 (`AL-408`)**, salvaged from the stderr logs of a repeat run that an environment interruption killed at 92 of 129 scorings (`AL-407`). Across 91 matched (judge, arm) pairs on identical arms: mean absolute delta 0.217, median 0.140, p90 0.430, max 1.710, signed mean -0.010 so no systematic drift, and 8.8 percent of scorings move past the 0.5 margin. The instability is one judge's, not the panel's: gemini-3.1 averages 0.323 (max 1.710) against gpt-5.6's 0.120 (max 0.290) and grok-4.6's 0.143 (max 0.290, n=6 and too thin to lean on). That is the third independent line against gemini-3.1. **Not closed**: these are per-leg means because that is what the log carried, so the per-criterion run-to-run spread is still unmeasured, and it needs a completed run with `journal.jsonl` now that the harness writes one. **Closed 2026-08-16 by W7 run 4**, the first run to complete in this environment. Measured per criterion over 84 matched (judge, arm) pairs, spread differs fourfold across criteria: `imagery` 0.071 to `choice_quality` 0.286 mean absolute delta, so the single 0.217 leg-mean figure described no criterion in particular. The semantics question this row asked is answered: TAU_STRUCT is a description of the catalog's lower quartile, not a fixed bar, and it may fall. Note the correction this row already carried stands: `scripts/check_promotion_bundle.py` does enforce TAU_STRUCT on the promotion path, so the value is load-bearing rather than documentation-only, and a future pass that moves it materially should be reported rather than absorbed silently. And the question filed as unestablished (`result.bindings` nullability) took fifteen minutes: it is `dict[str, str]`, never None, and the real switch is `contract is None`. Revised recommendation: **B, then D scoped to bound skeletons as a fail-closed interim, then E**, with the verified caveat that D empties the bound candidate pool in both 16+ long cells (0 bound-and-fillable, 2 bound-but-over, 2 unbound in each), turning a loud failure into a silent unselectability. **B and D landed 2026-08-19; the caveat carried into D did not survive being executed.** The review's caveat, which I repeated without measuring, was that D empties the bound candidate pool in both 16+ long cells and turns a loud failure into a silent unselectability. Run across all 18 offered cells at the lowest live ceiling (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`, 64,000): of the 28 (band, length, style) combinations holding any candidate, **0 are emptied**, 6 are narrowed but non-empty, and 22 are unchanged. The worst case is 16+/long/prose and 16+/long/gamebook going 4 candidates to 2, because D screens BOUND skeletons only and each of those cells retains its two contractless members, which chunk. Seven distinct skeletons are dropped in total, matching the corrected 7-exposed figure exactly: `the-skyrail-heist` (two 10-13/long cells), `the-year-of-four-banners`, `the-third-shift`, `the-salt-archive`, `the-tricameral-city`, `the-ashfall-expedition`, `the-pale-road`. The caveat was a claim about a pool, applied to a cell; the pool does empty and the cell does not. E remains open and is what actually restores those seven. E carries no security cost: the sentinel path runs post-fill on the pre-fill document and `merge_fill_batch` is a whitelist, so chunking a bound fill narrows what the model can change. **E landed the same day and D was therefore not shipped.** `fill_subset_bound.md` (``fill_subset.md`` plus the three bound blocks lifted verbatim from ``fill_bound.md``) and `build_fill_subset_bound_prompt` give a bound fill a chunked path, and `orchestrator.py`'s switch became `chunked = not is_fill_feasible(...)`: the `slot_bindings is None and` clause WAS the defect. Every batch carries the bound values, not only the first, since they are the story's names and a later batch without them re-invents the world the first one bound. With that in place the bound and unbound paths meet the same limit, so screening them differently at selection would now drop books that fill correctly: D was an interim against E not landing, E landed, and shipping both would have been a live defect rather than belt-and-braces. D's measurement survives as the regression guard `test_a_bound_skeleton_over_a_models_cap_is_still_a_candidate`, which records why the obvious narrowing is wrong so it is not re-added. Four stale code claims found and corrected on the way, three of them numbers a growing catalog outran: `chunking.py` said the largest skeleton needs ~87,200 tokens (99,906, `the-last-cartage`); `skeleton.py` said 131,072 clears every cell with 20 percent headroom (4.7 percent, and `AL-328`'s own justification for the margin no longer holds at the top of the catalog, which makes raising the constant a live question); `orchestrator.py` said 15 of 55 production skeletons chunk on the shipped backend (19 of 73); and `worker.py` said `personalizable_slot_ids` is empty for every contract on disk, which `the-midnight-museum.contract.json` falsified by declaring `HERO`. That last one is worth the note: the set is DERIVED from slot `kind`, not a top-level key, so grepping the JSON for the name finds nothing and is not evidence of absence. That book is under cap, so the chunked-and-personalizable combination is not live today. **Remaining under this row:** the two 16+/long cells still cannot reach their declared node ceiling at the band word target (655 against 750, 300 against 345) against the DEFAULT budget; chunking removes the per-model failure, not the per-cell ceiling, and that is still an owner call between lowering the ceilings and stating that the top of those envelopes needs a below-target word mean. | 3 | decision |

Proposed rule: ending titles unique within a story, cheap and reader-visible. (b) Nothing checks that an ending's `kind` or beats match where it sits in the graph: a court-flavoured outcome landed at a country fair, and `kind: capture` landed on a beat about being caught in a mirror array. PL-15 permits every kind at 16+, PL-24 only counts shares, and CG-4 is skipped on shells, so the shell gate is the last place this could be caught before a contradictory prompt reaches the fill stage. At 58 and 88 endings neither is something an author eyeballs. | 3 | unscheduled |
| UW-C304 | `AL-477`: **four unit tests froze a cell's skeleton membership as a literal list, so authoring into a covered cell broke tests that had no defect in them.** The 2026-08-18 fan-out added fourteen books and broke `test_skeleton_match.py::test_candidates_for_cell_matches_real_library_cell`, `::test_candidates_for_cell_matches_style_for_teen_band` and two in `test_authoring_plan.py`, all by asserting a frozen slug list for a cell. That is a tax on the project's central activity: every skeleton the catalog gains costs a test edit that carries no information. **FIXED**: the match tests now check `candidates_for_cell` against an INDEPENDENT read of the skeleton files (a small reimplementation of the three metadata fields the selector keys on, deliberately not a call to the function under test, which would be tautological), and the teen-band test asserts the property it is actually about, that prose and gamebook are disjoint cells, rather than their membership. The authoring-plan tests resolve the cell through the selector, since their assertions are about WHICH CELL the plan chose. | 5 | done |
| UW-C305 | `AL-477`: **the TAU_STRUCT drift `UW-C273` warned about has now happened and is measured.** Adding fourteen skeletons moved the anti-clone floor from **0.304707 to 0.283727**, a 6.9 percent WEAKENING in one authoring pass, and `scripts/calibrate_mutation_floors.py --check` fails until the baseline is recommitted. This is the exact mechanism `UW-C273` describes: the calibrator derives TAU_STRUCT as the 25th percentile of same-cell pairwise structural distance across the committed catalog, so authoring meant to strengthen the catalog dilutes the bar that keeps it varied, silently. The recalibrated baseline is NOT committed here, because committing it would resolve `UW-C273` by default in the "the floor may fall" direction, which is the owner's call and not mine. Options: adopt the ratchet (TAU_STRUCT may rise, may not fall without an explicit reviewed override), accept the fall as tracking a genuinely more varied catalog, or key the floor to something the catalog cannot move. Until it is ruled, `test_calibration_is_deterministic_and_committed_baseline_is_current` fails, which is the test doing its job. **RULED 2026-08-19 (owner): recalibrate.** TAU_STRUCT is now 0.283727, committed. What the recalibration actually shows, measured from the baseline's own stats block rather than from the headline number: same-cell pairs went 91 to 137, and the distance distribution did NOT shift down as a whole. The **median is flat** (0.379829 to 0.379198, -0.17%) and the **maximum ROSE** (0.580033 to 0.605791), so the catalog is more varied at the top and unchanged in the middle. What fell is the lower quartile, which is what TAU_STRUCT is defined as: p25 0.304707 to 0.283727 and p05 0.162541 to 0.148890. The minimum is untouched at 0.000469, the pre-existing near-clone pair that TAU_CELL's fixed 0.05 floor already rejects. **Every new pair is new-versus-legacy**, because the fourteen books went into fourteen distinct cells and no two of them share one, so the reading is that a book authored tightly to a cell's constraints lands closer to whichever legacy member also sits near those constraints than two legacy members do to each other. That is a real property of authoring to a strict bar, not a dilution of the catalog, which is what makes accepting the fall defensible here rather than merely convenient. | 3 | done |
| UW-C306 | `AL-482`, `AL-514`: **the drafting brief published a topology menu the cell could not use, and two independent Sonnet agents each picked a forbidden topology off it.** `reconvergence.capped_topologies` is a band-INDEPENDENT list, identical for every cell, and it reads as the set of topologies available subject to an in-degree cap. It is not: it names the topologies whose in-degree IS capped, several of which a given band may not declare at all. Measured against `validator/topology.py::BAND_TOPOLOGIES`: at 10-13 the brief named `gauntlet` and `time_cave` and the band admits neither; at 13-16 it named `time_cave`, also inadmissible. The brief published no per-cell admissible list at all, so an author had no authoritative answer anywhere in it. Both agents chose `time_cave`, one of them *because* it was the only listed topology no in-cell sibling had used, which is exactly the choice a brief should be steering; it built a full draft before `PL-29` blocked it. Two further gaps from the same run. (a) **The brief never mentioned `tier`.** Declaring any variable requires `metadata.tier = 2`; a tier-1 story that does is a blocking `L1-6`. That is the first thing to set on a stateful book and it cost the gamebook agent a whole iteration in the one cell where every book is stateful. (b) **`L1-7`'s depth cap is `nx.dag_longest_path_length`, not a reader-experienced depth**, while every other depth-flavoured rule an author meets is a shortest-path quantity. A single-choice detour that rejoins the spine adds a hop to the cap while adding nothing any one reader walks; six of them took a 35-hop story to 41. The prose agent found the mechanism only by reading `_branch_depth`'s source, and the finding message is a bare graph-wide scalar with no path and no node. **Fixed 2026-08-19**: the brief now publishes `topology.allowed_for_this_band` derived from `BAND_TOPOLOGIES`, a `depth` block naming the metric and the rejoining-detour trap, and a `state_budget.requires_tier_2` note; 54 new tests sweep all 18 offered cells and were confirmed to fail against the pre-fix brief. **Still open:** `L1-7`'s branch_depth finding should name the longest path or its terminal node, the way `CG-1`/`CG-3` name their run's members. That is a validator change rather than a brief change and is not done. | 3 | unscheduled |

The exposed reader is the child reading the chain straight through, which is the intended way to read it. Proposed fix: pairwise `diversity.grams.pairwise_overlap(include_choice_labels=False)` across chain members inside `validate_series`, where the relationship is already declared. Two decisions first: whether shared phrasing across a series is sometimes deliberate craft (a recurring refrain) rather than a defect, and where the bound sits, given the only anchors today are one converged pair at 215 and one unrelated pair at 2.84. Distinct from `UW-C93`/`AL-156`, which is same-skeleton sibling convergence; this is cross-skeleton, within-series. **Evidence base widened 2026-08-23** (`AL-566`): `scripts/check_corpus_convergence.py` now measures all 465 pairs of the committed corpus and labels each by relationship, giving `unrelated` median 0.59 / p90 2.52 / max 12.92 against the one `series` pair at 215.25, a 16.7x separation. The observer runs in `run_guard_battery.py` as a NON-gating row. **Both decisions ruled by the owner 2026-08-23** (`AL-568`): a series MAY repeat a phrase deliberately, and the bound is set on run LENGTH rather than volume, which permits a refrain with no declaration or allowlist because a refrain is short by construction. Shipped as validator rule SR-10 in `validate_series` (longest shared run <= 15 words; <= 2% of a book inside runs of 15+), blocking in `publishing/service.py::approve`, which is the only path in `src/` that sets `status="published"`. That is NOT the only way a row reaches that status, and the difference matters here: `scripts/seed_series_catalog.py`, `scripts/seed_staging.py` and `scripts/seed_dev_data.py` construct published rows directly, and the series seeder's `_assert_gate_clean` runs `validator.gate.run_gate` per BLOB while SR-10 is a per-CHAIN check that `run_gate` never reaches, so a series seeded that way is outside the rule. **Corrected 2026-08-23**: an earlier revision of this row called that gap structural. It is not. `seed_series_catalog.py` builds every blob of the chain inside one loop, so adding a chain-level `validate_series` call is about five lines, and `scripts/build_series_book.py::_cmd_series` already demonstrates the pattern. The real obstacle is that the seeded fixture FAILS the check: measured over its own blobs, `s_dev_ember_1` against `s_dev_ember_2` gives longest shared run 87 words at 100.00% coverage, because `_series_blob` is a template whose bodies differ only by one digit (`Ember Trail 1: a fork in the path demands a choice.` against `Ember Trail 2: ...`), and that template is duplicated into `seed_dev_data.py` (the canonical copy) and `seed_staging.py`. Two residuals, neither a blocker: (a) the already-authored `the-sunken-temple` now fails SR-10, so any future publish or catalog promotion of the brass-lantern chain is blocked until book 2's shared passages are rewritten; (b) the seed-script bypass above (`AL-574`), **owner-ruled 2026-08-23** to be closed by diversifying the fixture prose first and adding the chain-level check second, rather than by declaring seed scripts trusted input; the trusted-input reading was rejected because the fixture is reader-visible (`status="published"`, `visibility="catalog"`, plus a `StorybookAssignment` to a child profile) and because it would contradict the `#CRITICAL` marker already at `seed_series_catalog.py` naming `_assert_gate_clean` the sole check between seed-data regression and a child reading a rejected story. **CLOSED** on branch `fix/seed-series-chain-gate`: book 2 carries its own prose (`_BOOK_PROSE`, keyed by node role; book 1 unchanged) and `_assert_chain_clean` runs `validate_series` over the whole chain before any insert, in both duplicated copies. Longest shared run 87 -> 3 words, coverage 100.00% -> 0%. Gate function and CALL SITE are separately mutation-checked, since a test that calls the gate directly stays green when the call site is deleted. `seed_staging.py` needed no change: it seeds no series at all, which narrows the bypass from the three scripts this row originally named to two; (c) SR-10 measured UNSTRIPPED prose (`AL-575`): an ADR-023 sentinel's slot-id half is identical in every book binding that slot, so each sentinel inside a shared run added one spurious shared token, biasing SR-10 toward a FALSE block. **Closed 2026-08-23**: `grams.story_text` now calls `strip_sentinels`, which keeps one definition of a fill's prose across the blob path and the parsed-model path (`AL-563`). Provably a no-op against the current corpus, since all 40 committed fills measure clean. | 5 | done |
