| UW-J01 | `P7` auto-assign-on-publish, blocked on a `requested_by_profile_id` migration. Raised independently by the persona audit. **Unblocked 2026-08-15: the named blocker does not exist and probably has not since before this row was written.** The 2026-07-17 remediation plan recorded it as "auto-assign needs a schema migration threading `requested_by_profile_id` request -> concept -> storybook (no such link exists)", and this row inherited that wording. The link is complete in the shipped schema, under a different column name than the blocker named, which is the likeliest reason nobody re-checked: `story_request.profile_id` holds the requesting child profile and `story_request.resulting_storybook_id` holds the storybook the request produced (both `db/models.py`), and `publishing/service.py::approve` already calls `_stamp_resulting_storybook_id` and emits `EventType.RELEASED` at the exact point an auto-assign hook would run, so both ends are in scope of one function and no migration is required. Re-raised independently by the 2026-08-14 registration walkthrough ([onboarding-flow-walkthrough-2026-08-14.md](../qa/onboarding-flow-walkthrough-2026-08-14.md)) as its highest-severity finding: `api/library.py:425` hard-requires a `storybook_assignment` row and `api/assignments.py:328` is its only writer, so a guardian who requested a story for a named child still lands that child on an empty shelf. Scheduled as Wave 1 item 1a of [onboarding-overhaul-plan-2026-08-15.md](./onboarding-overhaul-plan-2026-08-15.md), which also carries the three constraints the implementation must reuse rather than reimplement (the K13 age-band ceiling in `assign_storybook`, assignment idempotency, and the `BOOK_ASSIGNED` event that drives the notification split in `UW-J23`). Note the remediation plan's own P7 row still carries the stale blocker text; it is left as the historical record and this row is canonical. | remediation, persona audit, registration walkthrough | 4b | unscheduled |
| UW-J23 | The `story_ready` notification claims a shelf the book did not reach. `notifications/registry.py:147` composes "It has been approved and published to your family library", which a guardian reads as "it is on the shelf", while the book is assigned to nobody; the correct-sounding sibling composer `_compose_book_assigned` (line 159) is the one that only fires on a real assignment. Rewrite the published-but-unassigned message to name the remaining step and link to the assign control. Stays worth doing after `UW-J01` lands, because auto-assign covers only the single-child request path: catalog books, second children, and profile-less requests still publish unassigned, and a rare wrong message is worse than a common one because nobody is looking for it. Wave 1 item 1b of the onboarding overhaul plan. **Scope grew 2026-08-15**: PR #720 put the same false promise on the marketing homepage ("Once you approve a book it lands on their shelf right away"). That sentence was corrected on branch `claude/minor-guardian-registration-flow-o0cmaf` and reworded to stay true after auto-assign ships, so it needs no second pass; the notification copy this row covers is still outstanding. Treat the landing footnote, the `story_ready` composer, and `UW-J26`'s Books page state as one claim that must agree. | registration walkthrough, PR #720 | 4b | unscheduled |
| UW-J24 | **Does an uninvited self-signup stay behind the admin approval gate?** Today it does: `api/onboarding.py` provisions every uninvited subject at `status="awaiting_approval"` and `require_principal` then refuses them everything including `GET /v1/me`, so a brand-new family waits on a platform admin they have no relationship with and no channel to reach, with nothing notifying either party in either direction. Coherent for an invite-shaped deployment; a dead end for anything resembling public signup, which is how the 2026-08-14 walkthrough hit it. The ruling decides the shape of the work, not merely its timing: keeping the gate makes it a notification and queue problem (`UW-J29`, `UW-J30`), while opening it makes it a compensating-controls problem, and the guardian-created invite path must keep its gate either way for the reason `api/onboarding.py` already documents (a guardian can name any address and would otherwise capture a stranger into their family on that person's first sign-in). Sits next to the still-open VPC method decision (`UW-N02`, `UW-A52`) and interacts with `UW-J25`: opening this gate while KWS verification stays off leaves no adulthood check anywhere in the product. Owner to rule: the project owner. Wave 0 item D1 of the onboarding overhaul plan. | registration walkthrough | 4b | decision |
| UW-J25 | **Is `kws_verification_required` turned on, and does a plain age affirmation ship regardless?** The flag defaults to `False` (`core/config.py:1159`), so on a default deployment the adulthood-verification step is skipped entirely and the only checks are the self-attestation checkbox on the consent form and the admin gate `UW-J24` may remove. The flag flip itself is counsel-gated and already tracked (`UW-M03`, `UW-N02`); this row exists to make the *combination* visible, because the two decisions have never been read together and the unsafe pairing is not obvious from either one alone. The separable half is the age affirmation before the OAuth button (`UW-J33`), which is not verification, must not be worded as though it were, and needs no counsel ruling. Owners to rule: the project owner with privacy counsel. Wave 0 item D2 of the onboarding overhaul plan. | registration walkthrough | 4b | decision |

Add the unassigned state and a direct assign affordance from the list. This is the recovery path for any guardian who already hit the gap, including everyone who hit it before auto-assign shipped, which is why it is not made redundant by `UW-J01`. Both are wrong under either outcome of `UW-J24`, so this does not wait on that ruling. Note the reason these read as true to their authors: on this deployment the single family's adult is dual-role, so "you approve" holds for them and fails for exactly the new families the funnel exists to attract. **Closed** on branch `claude/minor-guardian-registration-flow-o0cmaf`: the guardian's two real controls are named instead (passage editing, G6; per-child assignment, G16), the reviewer is named as ours rather than the reader's ("our safety reviewer", the possessive dropped in both the trust card and `ConsolePage.tsx`), the awaiting-approval copy names who actually acts and stops promising a message nothing sends (that promise is `UW-J29`), and the `/support` link this row asked for is on the waiting screen. Pinned by five tests across `LandingPage.test.tsx`, `ConsolePage.test.tsx`, and `GuardianAwaitingApprovalPage.test.tsx`, each asserting the old string's ABSENCE alongside the replacement's presence so deleting the section cannot pass; the two landing assertions were mutation-verified by reintroducing the old copy and confirming they fail. | registration walkthrough, PR #720 | now | done |

## Cluster K: documentation accuracy and compliance

Stale documentation is directed work: each item below either misleads a reader or misstates the
project's own inventory.

| UW-K01 | **Release blocker**: `docs/known-vulnerabilities.md` entries PYSEC-2022-42969 (`py`, via `interrogate` 1.7.0) and PYSEC-2026-89 (`markdown`, CVSS 7.5) are 68 days old with reassessment due 2026-07-20, now 8 days overdue. The OpenSSF release gate blocks releases for any vulnerability older than 60 days regardless of reassessment status. Closed by PR #464 (`757ff8e`): PYSEC-2022-42969 was withdrawn upstream and PYSEC-2026-89 does not affect the pinned `markdown` 3.10.2, so neither needed a dependency change. | UW-K20 | `docs/planning/` is the only directory in `docs/` exempt from front-matter validation, excluded twice over: the hook's `files: ^docs/(?!planning/).*\.md$` regex and a hardcoded skip at `tools/validate_front_matter.py:311-322`. The cause is that the validator has no `schema_type` concept and so applies the user-facing-docs schema to everything. Measured 2026-07-28: of 72 planning docs, 2 pass and 241 issues are raised, dominated by three systematic mismatches (planning docs carry an H1 the schema forbids, use `status: active` against an allowed set of draft/in-review/published, and use tags it does not know). The fix is a `planning` schema in the validator, not conforming 70 documents to a schema written for a different audience. Until then, `scripts/check_work_linkage.py` is the only automated gate over `docs/planning/`. | CI hygiene | unscheduled |
| UW-K21 | **No check verifies that an `AL-*` citation in prose resolves to the lesson it describes.** `scripts/check_work_linkage.py::_check_lessons_linkage` resolves `AL-*` tokens only where they appear inside cluster C of this register, so a citation anywhere else is never existence-checked and never semantically matched. Found when `generation-review-workstream-plan-2026-08-22.md` cited `AL-551` (the hand-copied-count lesson from PR #738) for the Stage 1 persist-gate defect, which is `AL-562` on branch `fix/stage1-persist-signal-coupling`. Both `check_work_linkage.py` and `check_lessons_log.py` passed tree-wide over that document. The same PR's own step-5 acceptance criterion forbids exactly this ("No `AL` citation in the brief or in code resolves to the wrong row"), so the criterion currently has no enforcer. Proposed: extend the linkage check to resolve every `AL-NNN` token under `docs/` and `src/` against the log, erroring on an ID that does not exist and warning where the cited row's Category does not match the citing context. Two lessons from the 2026-08-23 correction pass are held back until PR #742 merges, because the log requires consecutive IDs and #742 holds `AL-562`..`AL-568`: (a) an arithmetic identity over two computations proves nothing until both are shown to range over the same set, and (b) a same-day merge can land a review's subject before the review returns. **Unblocked 2026-08-23** by PR #742 merging as `37a08a60`, which landed `AL-562`..`AL-575` (not `AL-568` as estimated above); the two held-back lessons can now be appended above the current maximum. Noted while resolving the id collision this merge produced, and left for that pass rather than written here. | doc | unscheduled |

## Cluster L: live defects

These are not plan gaps. They are bugs found during the sweep, recorded here only so the register is
complete; each is filed as a GitHub issue.

| ID | Item | Issue | Status |
|----|------|-------|--------|
| UW-L01 | `machine.ts:108` wires `RESTART` to `start(story)`, the new-reader entry point, so a restart on a continuation read reseeds `var_state` from the book's declared initials and returns to `story.start_node`, discarding both carried series state and the continuation entry node. Root cause: the machine context keeps only `{story, reading, error}`, dropping the `entryNode`/`varState` pair that `ReaderPage.tsx:246` passed to `startContinuation`, so `reset` cannot reproduce a continuation even in principle. Canonical across ADR-024's deferred defect, debt `SL10`, diversity `B4`, and s5-handoff `B4`. **Fixed in [#648](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/648)**: `ReaderContext` now carries the `ContinuationSeed`, and both the initial-context factory and `reset` route through one `safeStart(story, continuation?)`, so a restart reproduces the continuation entry node and carried variables. Scope of the close: the machine can now reproduce a continuation, which is what this row said it could not do "even in principle". The seed is still supplied per session from router `location.state`, so a reload before the first save loses it; that remainder is tracked as the open half of `SL10`. | [#460](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/issues/460) | done |
| UW-L02 | `api/get_db_session` commits in the teardown half of a yield-dependency, which FastAPI runs after the response is sent, so a client could act on a `201` whose row no other connection could yet see. Was mitigated only by an 1100 ms newman delay that also serves as rate-limit spacing, so it could not be tuned for the race alone. **Fixed in [#647](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/647)**, split across two layers because neither can see both facts it needs: `middleware/unit_of_work.py` commits at the ASGI `http.response.start` message, which is the last moment before any byte reaches the client and the only place *where* to commit is observable; the dependency keeps the rollback, because it sits inside `ExceptionMiddleware` and is the only layer that can tell a raised `HTTPException(403)` from a deliberately returned `JSONResponse(404)`, which is *whether* to commit. `RequestUnitOfWork` settles exactly once so whichever half acts first decides. The dependency's commit survives as the fallback for a request that never emits a response start. | [#461](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/issues/461) | verify |
| UW-L03 | `Settings.rate_limit_redis_cooldown_seconds` (`core/config.py:342`, carries a `#CRITICAL: timing` tag and has unit-test coverage) was never threaded into `RateLimitMiddleware`, so the middleware fell back to a hardcoded `5.0` and `CYO_ADVENTURE_RATE_LIMIT_REDIS_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` had zero effect in any deployed environment. Found by the 2026-07-31 vulture calibration. Fixed 2026-07-31 in commit `bb42b308` (PR #520, "wire the rate-limit Redis knobs and close the UW-F22 dead-symbol findings"): `middleware/security.py:933` now passes `_settings.rate_limit_redis_cooldown_seconds` into the middleware config, alongside the sibling `rate_limit_redis_timeout_seconds` wiring that was already correct. | [#516](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/issues/516) | done |
| UW-L04 | **The readiness probe is unreachable from outside and returns a hardcoded 200, so it cannot report a database outage.** Found 2026-08-04 while executing `UW-F17`. Every backend router except `health` declares its own `/api/v1` prefix, and `frontend/nginx.conf` proxies only `location /api/`. `api/health.py` declares `APIRouter(prefix="/health")`, which is outside that prefix, so FastAPI's liveness and readiness handlers are not reachable through the ingress at all: `/api/health/live` returns a JSON 404 from FastAPI. Worse, the same nginx config defines `location /health { return 200 'OK'; }` for the frontend container's own probe, which shadows the path completely, so `GET https://cyo.williamshome.family/health/ready` answers with a two-byte `text/plain` `OK` from nginx (with a duplicated `Content-Type` header and `server: nginx`) while the readiness logic never executes. The consequence is not cosmetic: readiness is the check that proves database connectivity, so any external uptime monitor pointed at that URL reports healthy with the database completely down, and the R1 checklist recorded a "verified live 2026-07-07: both 200" pass on exactly that anti-oracle. `/api/v1/me` correctly returns `401 application/json`, confirming the proxy itself is fine and the fault is the prefix mismatch plus the shadowing stub. **Fixed 2026-08-04, not by the rename this row originally proposed.** A rename alone would have caused an outage: the production backend healthcheck is defined out-of-repo in homelab-infra `services/cyo-adventure/docker-compose.yml`, probes `/health/live`, exits non-zero on any status >= 400, and is wired to `depends_on: condition: service_healthy`, so this repo deploying ahead of that one would have marked the container unhealthy rather than merely mis-pathed. Delivered additively instead: `app.py` mounts the health router twice, `/api/v1/health/*` as the canonical schema-visible form and `/health/*` as a schema-hidden alias, with the alias's retirement precondition recorded in a `#CRITICAL` marker at the mount site. `middleware/security.py` widened its HTTPS-redirect exemption to both prefixes, since a missed prefix there surfaces as a 307 that a probe reads as a failure, not as a 404. `frontend/nginx.conf` narrows its own stub to `location = /nginx-health` and, critically, returns an explicit 404 for `/health` rather than deleting the block: deleting it would let the path fall through to the SPA `try_files` fallback and answer `200 text/html`, which is the same false pass wearing a different hat. Every probe in this repo (`Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `frontend/e2e-real/real-stack.ts`, runbook section 3) now asserts `content-type: application/json`, so a static stub cannot forge a pass. | [#609](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/609) | done |
| UW-L05 | **Production holds a family row with no users and no contents.** Found 2026-08-04 while executing `UW-F17` section 6, which needed the real family inventory to tell a genuine isolation check from a vacuous one. Production has three `family` rows: `3a152319` (the real family, 2 profiles, 6 assignments, 1 story request, 5 device grants, 3 reading states), `84b96700` (the E2E test family, 1 profile, 5 assignments, 4 grants, 0 story requests, 0 reading states), and `0ca7a109`, which has zero profiles, zero assignments, zero grants, zero requests, zero reading states, **and zero `user` rows pointing at it**. A family with no users is unreachable by design: every adult route resolves the caller's family through their `user` row, so nothing can read or write it and no console surface will ever list it. That is why it is recorded rather than urgent. It matters anyway for two reasons. First, guardian accounts are created by an empty `POST /v1/onboarding` (there is no signup form), so a family row with no user is the exact residue a partially-failed JIT provisioning would leave, and if that is the cause then the write is not atomic and a real guardian could hit it. Second, it inflates every family-count assertion, so a test that asserts "2 families" against production is already wrong and a test that asserts "3" encodes the anomaly as expected. **To do:** establish provenance before touching it, by checking whether `created_at` lines up with a known onboarding attempt or with an early manual seed, then either make the onboarding write atomic (transaction spanning family + user insert) or record it as a one-off seed artifact. Do not delete the row as the first action: while it exists it is the only evidence of how it came to exist. **Re-checked 2026-08-08: a fourth family appeared, and it is the more informative of the two.** `adb9e257`, created 2026-08-05 01:07 UTC, has zero profiles, assignments, grants, requests and reading states, but **one guardian `user` row**, unlike `0ca7a109`'s zero. That difference decides the provenance question this row was opened to answer: an empty family plus a user is what a *completed* empty onboarding leaves behind, whereas a family with no user is what a *failed* one leaves behind. So the two rows are probably not the same event, and the atomicity concern applies only to `0ca7a109`. Check whether `adb9e257`'s single user is a real person or a test sign-in before concluding either way; if it is real, the finding is a guardian who onboarded and never created a profile, which is a product observation rather than a defect. The real family also gained a third child profile in the same window, so **both** counts this register and the R1 checklist pin assertions to have now moved. Anything asserting a family count or a profile count against live production needs re-deriving; see the drift note in the checklist's Section 6. | (no issue) | unscheduled |
| UW-L06 | **Retire the un-prefixed `/health/*` alias.** `app.py` mounts the health router twice, `/api/v1/health/*` as the canonical schema-visible form and `/health/*` as a schema-hidden alias; the alias exists only so the out-of-repo production healthcheck keeps passing across the deploy. `UW-L04` recorded the retirement precondition in a `#CRITICAL` marker at the mount site but scheduled no work to act on it, so a permanent second mount entered the codebase with a retirement plan nothing tracks. Raised in review of [#609](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/609). **Open prerequisite, and the reason this is `blocked` rather than `unscheduled`:** homelab-infra `services/cyo-adventure/docker-compose.yml` probes `/health/live`, exits non-zero on any status >= 400, and is wired to `depends_on: condition: service_healthy`, so it must be repointed at `/api/v1/health/live` and redeployed *before* the alias is dropped. Dropping it first marks the container unhealthy rather than merely mis-pathed, which is the same outage the additive fix in `UW-L04` was chosen to avoid. Retirement is then three deletions plus their tests: the second `include_router` call in `app.py`, the `/health` entry in `_HEALTH_PATH_PREFIXES` (`middleware/security.py`), and the `location /health` 404 block in `frontend/nginx.conf`, whose explicit 404 exists only to stop the SPA `try_files` fallback answering `200 text/html` on the alias path. `TestLegacyHealthPathAlias` (`tests/unit/test_app.py`) and `frontend/e2e-prod/health-probe.spec.ts` both assert the alias's present behaviour, so they must be inverted rather than deleted; otherwise the retirement is assumed instead of proven. Also repoint the frontend container's out-of-repo external prober, which `frontend/Dockerfile` documents as curling bare `/health` and which this repo now answers with a 404 by design. | [#609](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/609) | blocked |
| UW-L07 | **Guardian profile resolution runs before the RLS context it depends on, so every guardian in production sees zero profiles.** Found 2026-08-08 while executing `UW-F17` against revision `631c0d8a` (v0.68.0). `api/deps.py::require_principal` calls `_resolve_profiles` (line ~545) roughly 36 lines before `apply_family_rls_context` (line ~581). `_resolve_profiles` selects from `child_profile`, an ADR-022 Tier 1 `family_scoped` table whose policy is `family_id::text = current_setting('app.family_id', true) OR current_setting('app.is_admin', true) = 'true'`. With the GUC unset, `current_setting(..., true)` returns NULL, the predicate is unsatisfiable, and the read returns zero rows, so `principal.profile_ids` is empty for every guardian. `GET /v1/profiles` then short-circuits on it (`api/profiles.py` ~374) and the console renders "No profiles yet" over a family that has three children. **This fails closed: it is an outage of the profiles surface, not a data leak**, and the underlying rows are intact. Confirmed live: the 2026-08-07 `e2e-prod` run captured `GET /api/v1/me` returning `"profile_ids":[]` and `GET /api/v1/profiles` returning `{"profiles":[]}` while the database held the profiles. **Why it stayed invisible until now:** RLS never applies to a table's owner, so before the ADR-021 cutover the API connected as `postgres` and the policy was inert; cutting over to `cyo_api` (`rolbypassrls=false`) is what activated the ordering bug. Any bug of this shape is undetectable pre-cutover, which argues for testing as the non-owner role rather than for testing more. **It is a recurrence, not a novel defect:** `_device_principal` (defined at deps.py ~625) already applies the context before its Tier 1 reads and carries a `#CRITICAL` comment naming the identical staging outage of 2026-07-18 to 2026-08-02; that fix was applied to the device branch only. **Sharper still, the file states the false invariant in its own words, one call site away from the bug.** Two `#CRITICAL` blocks assert exactly what `_resolve_profiles` falsifies: the block above the `apply_family_rls_context` call (deps.py ~570) reasons that "two of the three branches resolve without needing the context this sets: guardian/admin read the `user` row just above under the Tier 2 blanket policy", and the block inside `_device_principal` (deps.py ~674) states that `device_grant` "is the ONLY pre-principal read of one". Both are false the moment `_resolve_profiles` reads `child_profile` at line ~545. The fix must correct these two comments as well, or the codebase keeps asserting a disproven invariant beside the corrected code, which is how the device-only half fix survived review the first time. **Coverage gap that let it ship:** `tests/integration/test_rls_tier1_enforcement.py` covers the device branch (`test_device_principal_resolves_under_tier1_rls`) with no guardian equivalent exercising the real `require_principal` path as `cyo_api`. **Second-order finding, worth fixing with it:** the cross-family isolation test (`frontend/e2e-prod/guardian-books-and-isolation.spec.ts` ~184) asserts the E2E family sees zero story requests; `story_request` is `family_scoped` too, so under this defect that assertion is satisfied *by the bug*, with its rendered-heading positive control fully intact. Only the profiles half, which expects a non-zero count, catches the failure. An emptiness assertion over a Tier 1 table needs a non-empty companion assertion under the same policy; a rendered-heading control proves the page loaded, never that scoping ran. **Fix:** apply the context before `_resolve_profiles`, keeping the existing later call, correct the two `#CRITICAL` comments above, and add the missing guardian regression test at the `require_principal` level rather than at the query level. **Status caveat:** PR [#641](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/pull/641) is open and implements exactly this fix over the same two files, so if it merges before this row does, the `unscheduled` status and the Fix paragraph are already historical; the *deployed* claims above stay accurate either way, since they are conditioned on production serving the fix rather than on the fix merging. | [#639](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/issues/639) | unscheduled |

## Cluster M: external and owner-gated

Work this repository cannot complete on its own.

| ID | Item | Owner | Status |
|----|------|-------|--------|
| UW-M01 | Deploy the device-grant RLS fix to `:staging` once it merges, then re-run `e2e-staging.yml` to confirm the tier is green. **Fully re-diagnosed 2026-08-02; the original "rebuild the stale `:staging` image from `b29aa6b`" ask is refuted.** That premise fails two ways: every migration since `b29aa6b` is purely additive (no `DROP`/`RENAME COLUMN`), so a forward-migrated database cannot break older ORM code, and `trigger-image-build.yml` has succeeded on every merge through 2026-08-02. The tier's redness was **two independent faults**. (1) Three deterministic 500s (`/v1/notifications`, `/v1/review-queue`, `/v1/guardian/books`, the only three routes issuing a full-ORM `select(Storybook)`/`select(StorybookVersion)`) caused by the 07-30 migration stall, i.e. the database being BEHIND a current backend, the exact inverse of the original diagnosis. **Fixed** by repair run [30754078625](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/actions/runs/30754078625), confirmed in run [30757075720](https://github.com/ByronWilliamsCPA/cyo-adventure/actions/runs/30757075720). (2) The device-grant 401: `api/deps.py::_device_principal` reads `select(DeviceGrant).where(jti == ...)` to learn the grant's `family_id`, but `device_grant` is an ADR-022 Tier 1 `family_scoped` table and `apply_family_rls_context` only runs AFTER the principal is built, so the lookup runs with `app.family_id` unset, matches zero rows fail-closed exactly as `20260724120000_scoped_rls_tier1_family_scoping.sql` documents, and is reported as "device grant token failed verification". Not a secret, not a deployment fact: `docker logs` proves the SAME container minted (201) and rejected (401) in the same second. The comment at `deps.py:571` claims there is no chicken-and-egg, which holds for guardian/admin (the `user` table has a Tier 2 blanket policy) and for child (no DB read at all) but was never extended to the device branch, the only pre-principal read of a Tier 1 table. Latent since the 2026-07-18 Tier 1 cutover and invisible to every other tier because local and `e2e-real-*` connect as the owner, which bypasses RLS. **Code fix is in this repo** on `fix/device-grant-rls-lookup`: `_device_principal` now applies the RLS context itself, from the token's already-verified `family_id` claim, before the lookup. A `SECURITY DEFINER` bypass function was drafted first and discarded: it would have made app code depend on a migration-only database object (absent from the ORM `create_all` test fixtures) and could not deploy until that migration landed, reintroducing the very migrate-before-deploy ordering hazard that caused fault (1) above. Setting context from the claim needs no migration, deploys as pure code, and additionally binds the grant row to the family the token claims, which the `jti`-only lookup never checked. This row covers only the owner-side deploy and re-run that follows. | homelab-infra | unscheduled |
| UW-M02 | Naive-user session with real children. Gates UW-I01, UW-I02, and 15 unrun Track B scenarios. | project owner | blocked |
| UW-M03 | Counsel engagement: ADR-018 D1-D5, ADR-016 ring-2 granularity, ADR-023 OD-1 and OD-5. Long lead time; the roadmap already flags that this should start now. **Updated 2026-08-06: the ADR-018 leg is packaged and ready to send.** Every owner-side prerequisite is closed (D2 child-directed, D4 owner-drafts/counsel-reviews plus Safe Harbor sequenced counsel-first, D5 corpus constraint, all recorded in the ADR 2026-08-06; D1 and D3 were already decided), both rule-mandated D4 artifacts exist ([information-security-program.md](../compliance/information-security-program.md) published, [data-retention-policy.md](../compliance/data-retention-policy.md) drafted 2026-08-06), and the packet is assembled at [counsel-engagement-brief.md](../compliance/counsel-engagement-brief.md). Note the row covers D1-D5 now, not D1-D4: D5 (AI-training consent segregation) was added to the ADR 2026-08-01 and this row predated it. What remains is purely external: retain counsel and get rulings. The ADR-016 and ADR-023 legs are untouched by this update and remain unpackaged. | project owner | decision |
| UW-M04 | OpenRouter DPA execution (privacy-model Blocker 1a) | project owner | decision |
| UW-M05 | **Wyrmreach provenance conflict**: the series design and report live only on unmerged branch `claude/dnd-story-game-series-mqr9zy` with unsigned commits, and no `data/series/wyrmreach/` exists on `main`, yet the series is recorded as live in production. Resolve how published content reached production without a main merge before doing more Wyrmreach work. | project owner | decision |

| UW-M07 | **R2 cover bucket is publicly served, contradicting the code's own invariant.** `covers/storage.py` asserts as `#CRITICAL: security` that the bucket must have no public custom domain or r2.dev access bound to it, because covers are served only as short-lived presigned GET URLs. It did. Verified 2026-07-28: `GET https://cyo-bucket.williamshome.family/sk_ashfall_expedition/1.webp` returned 200 `image/webp`, 86,732 bytes, with no credentials. Because `cover_object_key` is deterministic (`{storybook_id}/{version}.webp`), anyone who could guess a storybook id could read any cover, including one still at `pending_review` under the H2 approval gate shipped in PR #469, so that gate bound the API read paths only. The bucket is no longer reachable at that hostname at all; the H2 cover gate is again a real reachability control, not only an API-surface one. | project owner | done |
| UW-M08 | **ADR-022 Tier 1 RLS is inert on production.** The prod backend connects as `postgres.cvrnaydpzijtszfbsraq`, and the `postgres` role has `rolbypassrls = true` (verified against the prod database 2026-08-02), so every `family_scoped` policy created by `20260724120000_scoped_rls_tier1_family_scoping.sql` is bypassed and the family-isolation backstop enforces nothing in production. The least-privilege roles the migration was written for (`cyo_api`, `cyo_worker`, both `rolbypassrls = false`) already EXIST on the prod project but are unused by the API. Staging is correctly cut over to `cyo_api`, which is the only reason UW-M01's device-grant defect surfaced at all: prod cannot fail that way because it enforces no policy. Remedy is the ADR-021 least-privilege cutover on prod, pointing the backend at `cyo_api` and the worker at `cyo_worker`. Do NOT cut over until UW-M01's code fix has merged and been verified on staging, or the same device-grant 401 will land on production the moment the role changes. **Resolved 2026-08-04, closed by the same cutover as `UW-A03`**: the precondition (PR #560 merged and green on staging) was satisfied before the production role switch, so the family-isolation backstop is now enforced live, not just present in migrations. | homelab-infra | done |

## Cluster N: external scope-comparison findings

Sourced from a 2026-08-05 comparison of three externally-authored, LLM-generated planning
documents (a project scope, a product/requirements document, and a commercial/revenue viability
report, all produced from a one-paragraph description with no visibility into this repository)
against the live project. Most of what those documents assumed was either already decided
differently here or is irrelevant to this project's actual delivery model; these rows are the
concrete mechanics and reference material worth retaining. Full detail, citations, and
field-level tables for each row live in
[external-scope-comparison-2026-08-05.md](./external-scope-comparison-2026-08-05.md), since the
source `.docx` files themselves live outside this repository and are not expected to persist. Each
row below restates enough of that document to be actionable on its own, which means the same claim
is written twice; **the comparison document is authoritative on any conflict**, and a correction to
a row's reasoning belongs there first.

| ID | Item | Phase | Status |
|----|------|-------|--------|
| UW-N01 | Adopt the external document's append-only entitlement-ledger design (grant/reserve/release/consume/expire/reverse events, never a mutable balance) as the starting design for commerce once Phase 8 (Capacitor + IAP) begins. No payment-processing or entitlement-ledger implementation exists in this repository today. Further informed by the companion Commercial and Revenue Viability Report's offer ladder ($9.99 one-time / $9.99 monthly / $69.99-79.99 annual test) and $0.90 per-story direct-cost stack. | 8 | unscheduled |
| UW-N02 | Feed ADR-018's still-open VPC method decision with the external document's cited sources (FTC COPPA Rule 90 FR 16918, codified at 16 CFR Part 312, effective 2025-06-23 with a general compliance date of 2026-04-22 that has **already passed** as of 2026-08-05; ROSCA; Oregon ORS 646A.293-295 and 646A.570-589) and its required consent-record field list. Decision, not implementation; waits on the counsel engagement [UW-M03](#cluster-m-external-and-owner-gated) already names. **Updated 2026-08-06: the citations above are now carried into [counsel-engagement-brief.md](../compliance/counsel-engagement-brief.md), which puts them to counsel marked as unverified-by-us and asks counsel to confirm them against the Federal Register text rather than relying on this repository's secondary sourcing.** That is the ADR's own stated caveat, so the row's remaining work is counsel's ruling, not further internal sourcing. The consent-record field list is still worth checking against the shipped `consent_*` columns once counsel confirms which fields are required. | 7 | decision |
| UW-N03 | Replace the hardcoded `CLOSED_VOCABULARIES` dict (`storybook/personalization_values.py`) with a versioned, schema-pinned `VariableDefinition`-style registry, so a historical story renders reproducibly after the variable catalog changes shape. Blocked on the same ADR-023 Stage A G1 STOP that blocks [UW-H01](#cluster-h-story-personalization-adr-023): do not start ahead of that re-plan. | 4b | blocked |
| UW-N04 | Widen the axe-core e2e a11y suite's tag scope from WCAG 2.1 AA (`wcag2a/wcag2aa/wcag21a/wcag21aa`) to WCAG 2.2 by adding **both** `wcag22a` and `wcag22aa`: axe's WCAG tags are additive per level, so `wcag22aa` alone covers only the 2.2 Level AA additions and would silently skip the Level A ones, consistent help (3.2.6) and redundant entry (3.3.7), which are the two criteria most relevant to consent and rights flows. In scope at AA: target size minimum (2.5.8), focus not obscured minimum (2.4.11), dragging movements (2.5.7), accessible authentication minimum (3.3.8). Out of scope for an AA target: 2.4.12 focus not obscured enhanced and 2.4.13 focus appearance, both AAA. Matches the external document's NFR-A11Y-01 baseline. **Updated 2026-08-11**: WCAG 2.2 scanning (both `wcag22a` and `wcag22aa`, exactly as this row specifies) now runs, but on the new weekly `accessibility-compliance-weekly.yml` job (ADR-029) rather than the per-PR gate, so this row is not closed. The owner ruled the per-PR `frontend-e2e` job should not grow scope or run time; widening the blocking gate itself, if ever wanted, is still this row's open work. | 5 | unscheduled |

Admin-only review does not scale headcount-wise to a multi-family product; the alternative is only as safe as the moderation-calibration work already tracked under `GS2`/`GS3`. Owner to rule: the project owner, jointly with whoever holds the safety-operator role at the time. | 7 | decision |

Do **not** adopt NFR-SEC-12's OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications as a control catalog: `assurance-spine.md` already classifies it, the API Top 10, and MITRE ATLAS as threat enumeration for corpus building, with the explicit note "Not control catalogs. Do not spine on them." Scoping refinement of an existing decision only; the actual gap closure is the existing Cluster E rows. | 5 | unscheduled |

It reproduces the 2026-07-20 resolved schedule verbatim rather than re-deriving it, settles the reading-progress row in favour of the resolved policy ("life of the active profile plus 30-90 days after deactivation" governs over the external 12-month-inactivity default), and adds all six omitted categories. **Three of the added categories carry no window and are written as "Not yet set, owner ruling required" rather than being given an invented number**: consent evidence, product analytics, and application logs. Financial records is marked not-applicable today (no payment data is collected) and needs a ruling before any payment feature ships. This row stays `decision` because those rulings are the remaining work. | 7 | decision |

Found while backfilling ADR-027's required Follow-on work section. The figure is a per-*story* total derived from public per-image rates, not a per-image price, and it carries a scale caveat: 8-12 scenes is the external document's assumption, while ADR-027's own pilot scope is 10-45 node images per book, so it must be re-derived against 10-45 before informing any expand decision. | 4b | done |
